# Youch! build pack

Everything an AI coding agent needs to rebuild the Youch! app UI in the Marker Proof style for SwiftUI (iOS): the brief, the design tokens, the real code that draws the UI kit and screens and the logo, with a prompt at the top that says how to use it. Generated by Looks Good.

## Part 0: How to use this file (prompt for the agent)

This section is addressed to you, the AI coding agent. Read it first and follow it exactly. It tells you what this file is, which part wins when two disagree, what to do in what order, and what to report when you finish.

**What this file is.** The build pack for Youch!: App UI, Marker Proof style, for SwiftUI (iOS). It holds the brand brief (Part 1), the design tokens (Part 2), the real CSS and reference markup that draw every UI kit component (Part 3) and every screen layout (Part 4), the logo (Part 6) and a checklist you must pass (Part 7). Everything you need is here; do not redraw anything from memory and do not invent values this file does not give.

**Authority order.** When two things in this file disagree, the earlier one wins:

1. Part 1 brief rules: what is allowed, where, and why.
2. Part 2 tokens: the only colours, type faces and spacing values you may use.
3. Part 3 component CSS values (`kit.css`): every colour, size, radius, border, shadow and type setting of every component.
4. Part 3 and Part 4 reference markup: illustrative. It shows structure and intent; class names are Looks Good's own, and the demo copy ("Brand refresh", "Maya Ortiz") is placeholder text.

**Steps.**

1. Save this file in the project root as `BUILD-PACK.md`, and read Part 1 completely before writing code.
2. Add `Theme.swift` from Part 2 to the app target. Every colour, font and shadow in the project comes from it.
3. Build each UI kit component from Part 3. `kit.css` is a specification of values (CSS pixels are points): read each component's colours, sizes, radii, borders, shadows, type and every state from it and draw it in SwiftUI with those values and the Theme.swift tokens.
4. For SwiftUI, Part 3 marks each component Native or Custom. Native rows: use the system control (`Toggle`, `Picker`, `.searchable` and so on), tinted with Theme.swift tokens only, and let the system supply its behaviour. Custom rows: draw them yourself from the Part 3 CSS values.
5. Assemble screens from Part 4: each layout names its blocks in order, its rules, and gives the reference markup of the phone screen. Spacing, the 44pt tap target and the type scale come from Part 1 and do not change per screen.
6. Patterns are out of scope for this build: it is an App, and app screens carry no patterns or art.
7. Place the logo from Part 6 at the sizes and positions Part 1 gives.
8. Finish with the Part 7 checklist: go through every item, fix what fails, and do not call the work done until all of it passes.

**Hard rules** (they repeat Part 1 on purpose).

- No colour outside the tokens. No gradient, opacity or tint that is not derived from them as Part 1 says.
- No art and no patterns in app UI: app screens are built from the UI kit only.
- The demo copy in the reference markup ("Brand refresh", "Maya Ortiz" and the like) is placeholder: replace it with the product's real text, but keep the structure and length discipline.
- Keep the 44pt minimum tap target and the phone type scale (sizes, line heights, weights) exactly as Part 1 gives them.
- Match both light and dark exactly as the tokens define them; the dark palette is designed, not inverted.

**Report back** when you finish: (1) the files you created or changed; (2) which Part 7 items pass, and for any that do not, why; (3) every place you could not match a Part 3 value or a Part 1 rule, and what you used instead; (4) any placeholder copy you left in.

## Part 1: Brand brief

The full AI brief for this brand, unchanged apart from its headings being one step deeper. It holds the rules: colours and where each is used, type, shape, states, motion, accessibility and what is allowed where. Where anything in this file conflicts with it, the brief wins.

### Youch! — build brief
Build the real product from this brief, not a mock-up. Sections are marked Locked, Guided or Free. Locked values are final: use only these colors, fonts, shapes and motion, and where one is missing use the nearest listed token. Guided sections give an intent and a range; stay inside them. Free means design it for this product: layout, screen structure and how many sections a screen has. Screens are composed from the UI kit below, one component for each need; do not invent new component styles, and do not copy the brand board's layout. Target: SwiftUI (iOS 17 and macOS 14 or later).
Youch!: Simple things, done well. Aesthetic: Marker Proof (Highlighted · Plain · Faithful; Highlighter pens on printed transcripts / Proofreader marks / Mac Notes and Mail / Gong and Zoom transcripts). Plain white paper cards under one hairline, where only the thing you chose is ever filled, like a highlighter on a transcript. Only what you marked.
Product: Youch!. Speech-to-text that just works. Locally. It tidies what you said and never rewrites it.

#### App UI rules (Guided)
Build every screen from the UI kit only. The brand shows up through colour, type, corners, edges, shadow and how each component is drawn. Never put the board's hero illustration, deck art, patterns, textures, decorative bands or header art into app screens. The logo appears at icon size in the top bar or on the welcome screen, nowhere else. Images appear only when they are content (a user photo, a product photo). Never introduce a colour that is not in the palette: destructive and status colours come from it too.
The brand board, deck and landing page show art for the brand's identity and marketing; none of it belongs in the app.
Screens are composed from the UI kit and the phone layout below: pick the component for the need, set text in the type scale, and leave the rest to the tokens.
Focus rings appear on keyboard focus only (`:focus-visible` on the web, the system focus on SwiftUI), never on tap or click; disabled controls take no focus and no press response.

##### How Marker Proof shows up in the app
- Corners: buttons 12pt, fields 12pt, cards square, badges pill.
- Surfaces: card unfilled; list rows unfilled; bottom sheet --canvas.
- Type: welcome title Manrope (display) 700, tracking -0.03em; section labels Inter (body) 400, uppercase, tracking +0.14em; button labels Inter (body) 600.
- Primary and selection: the primary button is --primary with a --on-primary label; the selected segment is --primary; the selected tab shows a 16x3pt --primary pill, near the bottom of the box, centred.
- Signature details: toggle: a 21pt mark drawn as --ab-muted with a diagonal --white at 70% stroke, vertically centred in the box, at the left; pie menu: a 152pt --ink at 3% dot with a 1pt solid --line edge, vertically centred in the box, centred; skeleton screen: a smooth fade of --canvas at 55% at 90° to transparent (a soft gloss, no hard line).

Where this aesthetic's colour rules and a component description disagree, the colour rules win.
Palette colours no app component draws (brand board and app icon only; they stay in `:root`): `--secondary` #dde1f2, `--sec-tint` #eff0f0 / #3b3c42, `--kit-stepper` #3b3a38.
- Primary budget: `Palette.primary` appears only where the UI kit draws it (Primary button, Secondary button, Text field, Search field, Checkbox, Radio, Toggle, Segmented control, List rows, Tab bar, Tabs, Split button, Floating button, Combo box, Listbox, Knob, Date picker, Navigation bar, Anchor links, Contextual menu, Ribbon, Pie menu, the highlight word), never as extra fills, text or rules. Selection, toggles, tabs and focus are drawn as their kit rows say, not in extra primary.
- Highlight word: use the brand's tagline unchanged as the welcome title (never reword it) and highlight its last word together with any full stop, wrapped in one span with `white-space: nowrap`; draw it as the Built Welcome screen does: text Palette.accentInk, a Palette.kitTitleTile band covering the lower 38% of the word's height (a hard edge, no fade), an underline in Palette.primary, 0.18em thick, 0.06em below the baseline.
- Text colours are the ones each UI kit row states; no other colours, tints or gradients on text.

#### UI kit (Locked)
Measured from the UI kit tab as Marker Proof draws it. Every control and container in the product is one of these components, drawn exactly as stated. Colours are tokens from Colors (a plain hex or rgba() is a fixed value the kit draws); "`--x` at N%" means `color-mix(in srgb, var(--x) N%, transparent)` and "at N% over `--canvas`" means `color-mix(in srgb, var(--x) N%, var(--canvas))`; sizes are points at the 390 x 844 reference (1pt = 1 CSS px); padding is CSS shorthand; exact shadow values are the `--shadow-*` tokens; (display) and (body) name the brand faces. "Other states" lists only what changes from the first state.
Native rows: use the system control and only tint it (accent, track, label font); keep its size, motion and behaviour. Custom rows: draw them exactly as described.

#### Product screens (Guided)
These are Youch!'s own screens, from its product sheet. What gets checked is the content ranking (1 is the most important), where the style's signature lands, and the fold budget; the layout itself is designed for the product from the kit's components, with no pixel positions given. Comps of these screens are viewable in Looks Good only.
Platform: Mac. Each screen is a Mac app window, 1280x800 (comps are 2560x1600 at 2x); content taller than the window is drawn in full with a fold line at 800.

##### History
- Job: Find a past dictation, see exactly what was said against what was pasted, and fix a word once so Youch! learns it.
- Primary action: Replay audio
- Chrome: Sidebar source list (History, Dictionary, Cleanup), History selected
- Content, ranked:
  1. Large title "History", highlight word "History".
  2. List group "Today" with 4 rows: "> Claude" (2:14 PM, 312 words · 2 min); "Dia" (1:02 PM, 88 words · 38 sec); "Mail" (11:40 AM, 141 words · 55 sec); "Weekly sync with Sarah" (9:30 AM, 4,812 words · 32 min).
  3. Card "Claude · 2:14 PM", with "312 words · 2 min", body "So the plan for Pramand is to ship the dictionary first. Then history, then the cleanup dial.".
  4. Card "What you said", body "~~um~~ so the plan for prominent is to ~~uh~~ ship the dictionary first, oh actually I meant Pramand. Then history, then the cleanup dial.".
  5. callout "Learned: prominent → Pramand. Youch! will now correct it in context.".
  6. The one primary button "Replay audio", with "2:08".
- Signature placement: the highlighter band on the selected row and on the fixed word.
- Fold budget: The list and the open dictation with its "What you said" fit in the 800 pt window.
- States:
  - Empty: not specified
  - Loading: not specified
  - Error: not specified
  - First run: not specified

##### Cleanup
- Job: Choose how much Youch! tidies, per app, with every level an enforced rule set that cannot add words.
- Primary action: Save
- Chrome: Sidebar source list (History, Dictionary, Cleanup), Cleanup selected
- Content, ranked:
  1. Large title "Cleanup", highlight word "Cleanup".
  2. Segmented control "Cleanup dial" with options "Light", "Medium", "Heavy", "Light" selected.
  3. Muted caption "Light removes fillers and fixes punctuation. Nothing else changes.".
  4. List group "Styles per app" with 4 rows: "Claude" (Light); "Dia" (Light); "Mail" (Medium); "Messages" (Medium).
  5. Toggle row "Faithfulness check", on, with "Every output word must come from what you said or the dictionary".
  6. Toggle row "Resolve spoken corrections", on, with ""oh, actually, I meant X"".
  7. Toggle row "Automatic lists and numbering", on.
  8. The one primary button "Save", pinned in a bottom bar above the home indicator or tab bar, always above the fold.
- Signature placement: the highlighter band on the chosen level.
- Fold budget: The dial, the per-app list and the toggles fit in the window.
- States:
  - Empty: not specified
  - Loading: not specified
  - Error: not specified
  - First run: not specified

##### Dictionary
- Job: Teach Youch! the names you say most by recording each one, and see what it has learned.
- Primary action: Record pronunciation
- Chrome: Sidebar source list (History, Dictionary, Cleanup), Dictionary selected
- Content, ranked:
  1. Large title "Dictionary", highlight word "Dictionary".
  2. Input field "Add a word", value "Pramand", placeholder "Type a name".
  3. Stat row "Pronunciations recorded" with Recorded: 3; Needed: 5.
  4. The one primary button "Record pronunciation", with "3 of 5".
  5. List group "Words" with 3 rows: "Pramand" (Heard as "prominent", "Promand", 3 recordings); "EndMean" (Learned from your edits, 1 fix); "Parakeet" (Heard as "para keet", 5 recordings).
  6. Muted caption "Fix a word in History and Youch! shows what it learned, with Undo.".
  7. Card "Words heard wrong this week", with "2", body "prominent, Promand".
- Signature placement: the highlighter band on the word being trained.
- Fold budget: The field, the word list and the learned callout fit in the window.
- States:
  - Empty: not specified
  - Loading: not specified
  - Error: not specified
  - First run: not specified

#### Phone layout and type (Guided)
| Role | Face | Size pt | Line | Weight | SwiftUI style |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Display | Manrope (`Manrope-Bold`) (display), tracking -0.03em | 34 | 41 | 700 | .largeTitle |
| Eyebrow | Inter (`Inter-Regular`) (body), uppercase, tracking +0.12em | 13 | 18 | 400 | .caption |
| Label | Inter (`Inter-Regular`) (body), uppercase, tracking +0.14em | 13 | 18 | 400 | .caption |
| Large title | Manrope (`Manrope-Bold`) (display), tracking -0.02em | 34 | 41 | 700 | .largeTitle |
| Title | Manrope (`Manrope-Bold`) (display), tracking -0.02em | 28 | 34 | 700 | .title |
| Title 2 | Manrope (`Manrope-Bold`) (display), tracking -0.02em | 22 | 28 | 700 | .title2 |
| Headline | Inter (`Inter-Medium`) (body) | 17 | 22 | 500 | .headline |
| Body | Inter (`Inter-Regular`) (body) | 17 | 24 | 400 | .body |
| Callout | Inter (`Inter-Regular`) (body) | 16 | 22 | 400 | .callout |
| Subhead | Inter (`Inter-Regular`) (body) | 15 | 20 | 400 | .subheadline |
| Footnote | Inter (`Inter-Regular`) (body) | 13 | 18 | 400 | .footnote |
| Caption | Inter (`Inter-SemiBold`) (body) | 12 | 16 | 600 | .caption |
| Tab label | Inter (`Inter-SemiBold`) (body) | 10 | 12 | 600 selected, 500 unselected | .caption2 |
- Each role is a `Font.brand<Role>` in Theme.swift (for example `Font.brandLargeTitle`), a custom font that scales with Dynamic Type through its text style.
- Phone chrome: the status bar takes 54pt at the top and the home indicator 34pt at the bottom; nothing tappable sits in either. The top bar is 44pt high, under the status bar. At rest it shows only the back button and icon actions, with the Large title below it in the content; the title appears in the top bar (Headline size) only once the Large title has scrolled under it.
- Title colours: welcome title --ink, screen titles --ink, eyebrows --ab-muted.
- Layout: 20pt side margins on every screen. Spacing comes from 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 40, 48pt only; 28pt between sections. Sizes: buttons 52pt, fields 48pt, list rows 52pt with one line and 59pt with a detail line, card insides as the Home card in the sample screens, tab bar 83pt, nothing tappable under 44pt.
- Support Dynamic Type; never let a word split or a label truncate; one Large title per screen.

#### Shape and materials (Locked)
Corners, edges and shadows are the UI kit rows above; use `style: .continuous` on SwiftUI rounded shapes.
CSS to SwiftUI: a box-shadow blur B is `.shadow(radius: B/2)`; `inset` shadows use the `Recessed` modifier in Theme.swift; `linear-gradient(135deg, ...)` is `LinearGradient(startPoint: .topLeading, endPoint: .bottomTrailing)`; `rgba()` is `Color(.sRGB, red:, green:, blue:, opacity:)`; `backdrop-filter` is a SwiftUI `Material`.

#### Light and dark (Locked)
The default look is light, as the sample screens draw it. Dark is used only when the system asks for it: adaptive colours from Theme.swift follow the system appearance. The dark values are designed, not inverted. Text on the canvas uses `--ink` / `--muted`, with each mode's value from the Colors table.

#### States (Locked)
| State | Colour | Visual |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pressed and disabled | as each UI kit row states | The "Other states" column of the UI kit is the one definition; nothing else changes on press. Disabled controls take no press response. |
| Focus | `Palette.primary` where it holds 3:1 on the canvas, else `Palette.ink` | Keyboard focus on any control: a 2pt ring with a 2pt gap, following the control's corners; exactly as the "focus" state of the Primary and Secondary button rows in the UI kit draws it. Shown on keyboard focus only (`:focus-visible` on the web), never on tap or click; never removed. Text fields show their own focus state from the UI kit instead. |
| Empty | `Palette.ink` headline, `Palette.muted` copy | The Empty state row of the UI kit. |
| Loading | `Palette.line` track, `Palette.primary` fill | Indeterminate bar, or skeleton blocks in `Palette.line` easing between 100% and 60% opacity every 1.2 s. Show it after 300 ms of waiting, with a text label for screen readers. |
| Error | `Palette.error` | The Text field row's error state, with the message under the field. |

#### Motion (Guided)
- Durations: fast 90 ms (press, toggle), base 140 ms (color, selection, shadow), slow 220 ms (sheets, page changes). Easing: ease-out (`Motion.press`, `Motion.settle` in Theme.swift).
- Reduce Motion: Replace slides and fades with an instant state swap; colour changes stay. State changes stay; only the movement goes. Nothing else animates idly or loops except loading indicators.

#### Accessibility (Locked)
- Contrast kept (light | dark): ink on canvas 17.0 | 16.1 (AAA); muted on canvas 4.8 | 6.6 (AA); on-primary on primary 5.9 | 5.9 (AA); on-accent on accent 12.3 | 12.3 (AAA); accent-ink on canvas 17.0 | 11.3 (AAA); error on canvas 17.0 | 16.1 (AAA). Keep every text pair at 4.5:1 or better (3:1 for the accent-ink highlight word); do not swap tokens in a way that breaks them.
- Every tappable target is at least 44x44pt even when the visible shape is smaller, with 8pt between neighbours. Text scales: Dynamic Type through the `Font.brand*` styles, `@ScaledMetric` for icon sizes and paddings; layouts reflow at the largest accessibility size (stack, wrap, never truncate a primary label).
- Reduce Transparency (`accessibilityReduceTransparency`): translucent fills in the kit (overlay, skeleton screen) become opaque. Increase Contrast (`colorSchemeContrast == .increased`): Hairlines become 2pt `Palette.ink`, `Palette.muted` text becomes `Palette.ink`, tints get a 1pt `Palette.ink` edge.
- Reduce Motion (`accessibilityReduceMotion`): see Motion. Never color alone: selection, status and errors also use an icon, text or shape; icon-only buttons carry an accessibility label.

#### Icons (Locked)
1.75px strokes, round caps, 20px; the selected icon sits on a highlighter band. SwiftUI: SF Symbols at weight `.regular`, monochrome rendering; template-rendered SVG assets only for glyphs SF Symbols lacks.
- Sizes as the sample screens draw them: 24pt tab bar icons over their 10pt labels; 20pt icons in icon buttons, centred in a 44x44pt hit area; 18pt icons inside the 32pt feature-row chips; 16pt chevrons in the trailing slot of rows, never a text glyph (no ›, > or →); 24pt anywhere else.
- Glyphs the kit uses (draw each once, same stroke): back, more, notifications (bell), search, chevron right, chevron down, check, plus, info, success, warning, error, inbox (empty state), home, list, heart, user.

#### Fonts to bundle (Locked)
- Manrope: 400 (UI kit) as `Manrope-Regular`, 400 italic (UI kit) as `Manrope-Italic`, 700 (Display, Large title, Title, Title 2, UI kit) as `Manrope-Bold`.
- Inter: 400 (Eyebrow, Label, Body, Callout, Subhead, Footnote, UI kit) as `Inter-Regular`, 500 (Headline, UI kit) as `Inter-Medium`, 600 (Caption, Tab label, UI kit) as `Inter-SemiBold`, 700 (UI kit) as `Inter-Bold`.
- Geist Mono: 600 (UI kit) as `GeistMono-SemiBold`.
- System faces, no file to bundle: -apple-system.
- Bundle exactly these weights and no others; the type scale uses no more. SwiftUI: use static TTFs (the Google Fonts download, `static/` folder), add them to the target and list them under `UIAppFonts` (iOS) or `ATSApplicationFontsPath` (macOS). The names above are PostScript names.

#### Signature and product imagery (Guided)
- Embedded product screenshots, app icons and product imagery keep their own colours. They are exempt from the canvas and colour coverage budget and from the accent and primary limits; the studio frame around them (surfaces, captions, borders) follows the brand rules.
- Coverage and whitespace targets apply to the brand's own surfaces, not to product pages that showcase a product's own visual system.

#### Do and don't (Guided)
- Do: Fill only what is chosen: selected, current, open, focused or the one main action.
- Do: Strike a disabled control through with the diagonal hatch rather than greying it.
- Don't: Fill a card or row at rest.
- Don't: Draw errors in stock red: use ink, an icon and words.

#### Theme.swift (Locked)
Add this file to the target as is. Build every view from `Palette`, `Font.brand*`, `Metrics`, `Spacing`, `Motion`, `ThemeButtonStyle`, `themeSurface()`, `themeField()` and `themePage()`; never hard-code a color, size or duration. It covers the pressed, disabled, focus and error states; empty and loading follow the States table.
```swift
// Theme.swift: Youch!, Marker Proof. Generated by Looks Good; change the brief, not these values.
import SwiftUI

extension Color {
    init(hex: UInt32, opacity: Double = 1) {
        self.init(.sRGB, red: Double((hex >> 16) & 0xFF) / 255, green: Double((hex >> 8) & 0xFF) / 255, blue: Double(hex & 0xFF) / 255, opacity: opacity)
    }
    /// Adaptive color: follows the system light or dark appearance.
    init(light: UInt32, dark: UInt32, lightOpacity: Double = 1, darkOpacity: Double = 1) {
        #if canImport(UIKit)
        self = Color(UIColor { $0.userInterfaceStyle == .dark ? UIColor(Color(hex: dark, opacity: darkOpacity)) : UIColor(Color(hex: light, opacity: lightOpacity)) })
        #else
        self = Color(NSColor(name: nil) { $0.bestMatch(from: [.darkAqua, .aqua]) == .darkAqua ? NSColor(Color(hex: dark, opacity: darkOpacity)) : NSColor(Color(hex: light, opacity: lightOpacity)) })
        #endif
    }
}

enum Palette {
    static let canvas = Color(light: 0xF3F3F0, dark: 0x1C1D21)   // The screen background
    static let ink = Color(light: 0x101116, dark: 0xFAFAF7)   // Default text and icons
    static let muted = Color(light: 0x6B6B6D, dark: 0xA1A2A1)   // Secondary text from the palette
    static let line = Color(light: 0xD1D1CF, dark: 0x3D3E41)   // Hairlines, dividers, resting input borders
    static let primary = Color(light: 0x8290C7, dark: 0x8290C7)   // Main action
    static let accent = Color(light: 0xF6CF6B, dark: 0xF6CF6B)   // One spark
    static let accentInk = Color(light: 0x101116, dark: 0xF6CF6B)   // The accent as text when it reaches 3:1 on the canvas; otherwise the in
    static let priTint = Color(light: 0xE5E7EB, dark: 0x282B35)   // Quiet primary wash: selected background, hover on primary-linked items
    static let error = Color(light: 0x101116, dark: 0xFAFAF7)   // Errors and destructive actions; never a stock red
    static let kitPrimary = Color(light: 0x68739F, dark: 0x68739F)   // Measured from the UI kit in light and dark
    static let white = Color(light: 0xFFFFFF, dark: 0xFFFFFF)   // Pure white, where the UI kit draws it
    static let kitPrimary-2 = Color(light: 0x414245, dark: 0xC0C1C0)   // Measured from the UI kit in light and dark
    static let kitField = Color(light: 0x101116, dark: 0x8290C7)   // Measured from the UI kit in light and dark
    static let kitField-2 = Color(light: 0xC62F2F, dark: 0xFF7B72)   // Measured from the UI kit in light and dark
    static let abMuted = Color(light: 0x545557, dark: 0xAAABAA)   // A colour of Marker Proof's UI kit
    static let black = Color(light: 0x000000, dark: 0x000000)   // Pure black, where the UI kit draws it
    static let kitToggle = Color(light: 0x4F5777, dark: 0xB8C0DD)   // Measured from the UI kit in light and dark
    static let successIcon = Color(light: 0x81838F, dark: 0xDDE1F2)   // Success callout icon, 3:1 on its tint
    static let warningIcon = Color(light: 0x8F7A45, dark: 0xF6CF6B)   // Warning callout icon, 3:1 on its tint
    static let primaryText = Color(light: 0x606A92, dark: 0x8290C7)   // The primary as text and small icons (text buttons, tinted icons), shad
    static let kitToastText = Color(light: 0x6D6D6C, dark: 0x8E8E90)   // Text of this component, kept at 4.5:1 on its fill in both modes
    static let kitStepperText = Color(light: 0x3B3A38, dark: 0x898988)   // Text of this component, kept at 4.5:1 on its fill in both modes
    static let kitTitleTile = Color(light: 0xF6CF6B, dark: 0x000000, darkOpacity: 0)   // Measured from the UI kit in light and dark
    static let kitFeature = Color(light: 0x5A6489, dark: 0x8290C7)   // Measured from the UI kit in light and dark
    static let onPrimary = Color(light: 0x141414, dark: 0x141414)   // Named in this brief (a colour of the style)
    static let kitStepper = Color(light: 0x3B3A38, dark: 0x3B3A38)   // Named in this brief (a colour of the style)
    static let onAccent = Color(light: 0x141414, dark: 0x141414)   // text or icon on the fill
    static let onError = Color(light: 0xF3F3F0, dark: 0x1C1D21)   // text or icon on the fill
}

extension Font {
    static let brandDisplay = Font.custom("Manrope-Bold", size: 34, relativeTo: .largeTitle)
    static let brandEyebrow = Font.custom("Inter-Regular", size: 13, relativeTo: .caption)
    static let brandLabel = Font.custom("Inter-Regular", size: 13, relativeTo: .caption)
    static let brandLargetitle = Font.custom("Manrope-Bold", size: 34, relativeTo: .largeTitle)
    static let brandTitle = Font.custom("Manrope-Bold", size: 28, relativeTo: .title)
    static let brandTitle2 = Font.custom("Manrope-Bold", size: 22, relativeTo: .title2)
    static let brandHeadline = Font.custom("Inter-Medium", size: 17, relativeTo: .headline)
    static let brandBody = Font.custom("Inter-Regular", size: 17, relativeTo: .body)
    static let brandCallout = Font.custom("Inter-Regular", size: 16, relativeTo: .callout)
    static let brandSubhead = Font.custom("Inter-Regular", size: 15, relativeTo: .subheadline)
    static let brandFootnote = Font.custom("Inter-Regular", size: 13, relativeTo: .footnote)
    static let brandCaption = Font.custom("Inter-SemiBold", size: 12, relativeTo: .caption)
    static let brandTablabel = Font.custom("Inter-SemiBold", size: 10, relativeTo: .caption2)
    // Display and Title: tracking -0.03em. Apply .textCase(.uppercase) and .tracking(...) at the call site.
}

enum Metrics {
    static let radiusControl: CGFloat = 12
    static let radiusField: CGFloat = 12
    static let radiusCard: CGFloat = 0
    static let outline: CGFloat = 1
    static let hit: CGFloat = 44   // minimum tappable size
    static let margin: CGFloat = 20   // side margin of every screen
    static let buttonHeight: CGFloat = 52, fieldHeight: CGFloat = 48, rowHeight: CGFloat = 52, cardPadding: CGFloat = 20
}

enum Spacing {
    static let xs: CGFloat = 4, s: CGFloat = 8, m: CGFloat = 16, l: CGFloat = 24, xl: CGFloat = 32
    static let sm: CGFloat = 12, ml: CGFloat = 20, xxl: CGFloat = 40, xxxl: CGFloat = 48
}

enum Motion {
    static let fast: Double = 0.09, base: Double = 0.14, slow: Double = 0.22
    static let press: Animation = .easeOut(duration: fast)
    static let settle: Animation = .easeOut(duration: base)
}

struct ThemeButtonStyle: ButtonStyle {
    @Environment(\.isEnabled) private var isEnabled
    @Environment(\.accessibilityReduceMotion) private var reduceMotion
    func makeBody(configuration: Configuration) -> some View {
        let shape = RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: Metrics.radiusControl, style: .continuous)
        let down = configuration.isPressed
        return configuration.label
            .font(.brandHeadline)
            .foregroundStyle(isEnabled ? Palette.onPrimary : Palette.muted)
            .padding(.horizontal, Spacing.l)
            .frame(minHeight: Metrics.hit)
            .background(isEnabled ? Palette.primary : Palette.line, in: shape)
            .opacity(down ? undefined : 1)
            .offset(x: down ? 0 : 0, y: down ? 1 : 0)
            .animation(reduceMotion ? nil : Motion.press, value: down)
    }
}

struct ThemeSurface: ViewModifier {
    func body(content: Content) -> some View {
        let shape = RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: Metrics.radiusCard, style: .continuous)
        return content
            .padding(Spacing.m)
            .background(Palette.canvas, in: shape)
            .overlay(shape.strokeBorder(Palette.line, lineWidth: Metrics.outline))
    }
}

struct ThemeField: ViewModifier {
    var focused = false
    var failed = false
    func body(content: Content) -> some View {
        let shape = RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: Metrics.radiusField, style: .continuous)
        return content
            .font(.brandBody)
            .padding(.horizontal, Spacing.m)
            .frame(minHeight: Metrics.hit)
            .background(Palette.canvas, in: shape)
            .overlay(shape.strokeBorder(failed ? Palette.error : Palette.line, lineWidth: failed ? 2 : Metrics.outline))
            .overlay(shape.inset(by: -4).stroke(Palette.primary, lineWidth: focused ? 3 : 0))
            .overlay(shape.inset(by: -6).stroke(Palette.ink, lineWidth: focused ? 1 : 0))
    }
}

extension View {
    func themeSurface() -> some View { modifier(ThemeSurface()) }
    func themeField(focused: Bool = false, failed: Bool = false) -> some View { modifier(ThemeField(focused: focused, failed: failed)) }
    /// Page background and default ink; put it on the root view.
    func themePage() -> some View { background(Palette.canvas.ignoresSafeArea()).foregroundStyle(Palette.ink) }
}
```

#### App icon (Locked)
A white rounded tile with one highlighted word-bar in the primary. Deliver a 1024x1024 master without transparency or pre-rounded corners (the system masks it), a dark variant on `#1c1d21` and a tinted variant (glyph only, one color), and derive every other size from the master.

## Part 2: Design tokens

These are the brand's tokens: use them as given, and use nothing else for colour, type or spacing. The first block is the same file "Save + files" writes; the last block holds the values of every custom property the Part 3 CSS reads, resolved on the kit root, so every `var(--x)` in Part 3 has a value here.

### Theme.swift

```swift
// Theme.swift: Youch!, Marker Proof. Generated by Looks Good; change the brief, not these values.
import SwiftUI

extension Color {
    init(hex: UInt32, opacity: Double = 1) {
        self.init(.sRGB, red: Double((hex >> 16) & 0xFF) / 255, green: Double((hex >> 8) & 0xFF) / 255, blue: Double(hex & 0xFF) / 255, opacity: opacity)
    }
    /// Adaptive color: follows the system light or dark appearance.
    init(light: UInt32, dark: UInt32, lightOpacity: Double = 1, darkOpacity: Double = 1) {
        #if canImport(UIKit)
        self = Color(UIColor { $0.userInterfaceStyle == .dark ? UIColor(Color(hex: dark, opacity: darkOpacity)) : UIColor(Color(hex: light, opacity: lightOpacity)) })
        #else
        self = Color(NSColor(name: nil) { $0.bestMatch(from: [.darkAqua, .aqua]) == .darkAqua ? NSColor(Color(hex: dark, opacity: darkOpacity)) : NSColor(Color(hex: light, opacity: lightOpacity)) })
        #endif
    }
}

    static let ink = Color(light: 0x101116, dark: 0xFAFAF7)   // Default text and icons

    static let accent = Color(light: 0xF6CF6B, dark: 0xF6CF6B)   // One spark

    static let priTint = Color(light: 0xE5E7EB, dark: 0x282B35)   // Quiet primary wash: selected background, hover on primary-linked items
```

### Resolved custom properties read by the kit CSS

Set these on the kit root (the element carrying class `ab sb-ab`) before the Part 3 CSS applies. The first block is the light look; the second lists only the values that differ in dark, scoped by `data-mode`.

